<|begin_of_box|>Step-by-step reasoning. The target nodes are B and M. To disconnect them, I must identify all paths between them and find the minimal set of edges whose removal breaks all these paths. 

First, I list all possible paths from B to M:
1. B → H → E → M (edges: (B,H), (H,E), (E,M))
2. B → C → I → J → M (edges: (B,C), (C,I), (I,J), (J,M))
3. B → C → I → P → J → M (edges: (B,C), (C,I), (I,P), (P,J), (J,M))
4. B → N → O → P → J → M (edges: (B,N), (N,O), (O,P), (P,J), (J,M))

Next, I analyze which edges can break all paths. Removing a single edge is insufficient because:
- Removing (E,M) breaks path 1 but leaves paths 2–4 intact.
- Removing (J,M) breaks paths 2–4 but leaves path 1 intact.
- Removing other edges (e.g., (H,E), (I,J)) also leaves at least one path intact.

Thus, I need at least two edges. I test pairs:
- Removing (E,M) and (J,M): 
  - Path 1 is broken by (E,M).
  - Paths 2–4 are broken by (J,M).
- Removing (H,E) and (J,M): 
  - Path 1 is broken by (H,E).
  - Paths 2–4 are broken by (J,M).

Both pairs disconnect B and M, and no smaller set works. Therefore, the minimal cut set size is 2.

Final Answer: Edges to remove: 2, Cut Set: [(E, M), (J, M)]<|end_of_box|>
